FOUNDATIONS LAW
PART 1: GENERAL
Title
1.
This Law may be cited as the "Foundations Law 2018" or "this Law".
2.
Legislative authority
This Law is made by the Ruler.
Application of the Law
3.
This Law applies in the jurisdiction of the Dubai International Financial Centre.
Scope of the Law
4.
This Law applies to:
any Foundation established in accordance with this Law;
(a)
any Recognised Foreign Foundation to the extent stated in Article 62; or
(b)
any Foreign Foundation which is established in another jurisdiction which has transferred its
(c)
registration to the DIFC in accordance with this Law.
Date of enactment
5.
This Law is enacted on the date specified in the enactment notice in respect of this Law.
Commencement
6.
This Law comes into force on the date specified in the enactment notice in respect of this Law.
7.
Interpretation
Schedule 1 contains:
(1)
interpretative provisions which apply to this Law; and
(a)
a list of defined terms used in this Law.
(b)
Schedule 2 contains provisions relating to the application of the Arbitration Law.
(2)
Schedule 3 contains prescribed fines for contravention of this Law.
(3)
8.
Administration of this Law
This Law is administered by the Registrar.
Regulations
9.
The Board of Directors of the DIFCA may make Regulations to facilitate the administration of, or
(1)
further the objects of, this Law.
Without limiting the generality of Article 9(1), such Regulations may be made in relation to:
(2)
the objectives, powers or functions of the Registrar under this Law;
(a)
forms, procedures, notice and requirements under this Law;
(b)

FOUNDATIONS LAW
PART 2: NATURE OF A DIFC FOUNDATION
Nature of a Foundation
10.
A Foundation is a body corporate with a legal personality separate from that of its Founder(s) and
(1)
any other person.
A Foundation has the capacity, rights and privileges of a natural person. The validity of an act done
(2)
by a Foundation shall not be called into question on the ground of lack of capacity by reason of
anything in its Charter or By-Laws.
The property of a Foundation is not held by it upon trust for any other person.
(3)
A Founder has such rights (if any) in respect of a Foundation as provided for in its By-laws.
(4)
A person specified in the By-laws (other than a Founder, a member of the Council, the Registered
(5)
Agent and any Guardian) has such rights (if any) in respect of a Foundation as provided for in its
By-laws.
Any rights a person may have in respect of a Foundation may be assigned to some other person, if
(6)
its By-laws so provide.
If rights are assigned under Article 10(6), the person assigning the rights must within a period of
(7)
thirty (30) days provide a copy of the assignment to the Registered Agent or, if there is no Registered
Agent, to the Registrar. A person who fails to comply with this requirement is liable to a fine, as set
out in Schedule 3.
11.
Duration of a Foundation
A Foundation may, but need not, be established for a fixed period or for a specified limited period,
(1)
provided that it may be dissolved at an earlier time in accordance with the provisions of this Law.
If a Foundation is to be wound up and dissolved upon:
(2)
the happening of some event; or
(a)
the expiration of a fixed period of time,
(b)
the details of the event or period must be specified in its By-laws.
(c)
If a person is to have the right to wind up and dissolve a Foundation, the details of such right must
(3)
be specified in its By-laws, subject to any right arising under any applicable law.
12.
Objects and categories of Foundations
A Foundation's objects:
(1)
must be certain, reasonable and possible; and
(a)
must not be unlawful or contrary to public policy in the DIFC.
(b)
A Foundation may be established for:
(2)
objects which are exclusively charitable; or
(a)
one or more of the following:
(b)
objects which are not exclusively charitable; or
(i)
objects to benefit persons by name, category or class.
(ii)
3

any other Person with Sufficient Interest.
13.

Subject to Article 13(2), all matters arising in regard to a Foundation or in regard to any disposition
(1)
of property to or by a Foundation, including questions as to:
the capacity of a Founder or Contributor;
(a)

(2)

14.
Limitations in Foreign Law
Without limiting the generality of Article 13(1), no disposition of property to a Foundation that is
(1)
valid under the laws of the DIFC is void, voidable, liable to be set aside or defective in any manner
by reference to a Foreign Law; nor is the capacity of any Founder in relation to the Foundation or
disposition to be questioned nor is the Foundation or any other person to be subjected to any liability
or deprived of any power or right, by reason that:
the laws of any foreign jurisdiction prohibit or do not recognise the concept of a
(a)
foundation;
the disposition voids or defeats any rights, claims or interests conferred by Foreign Law
(b)
upon any person by reason of a Personal Relationship to a Founder or any other person
related to the Foundation or by way of Heirship Rights or contravenes any rule of Foreign
Law or any foreign, judicial or administrative order, arbitration award or action intended
to recognise, protect, enforce or give effect to any such rights, claims or interests; or
the Foreign Law or foreign judicial or administrative order or arbitration award or imposes
(c)
any obligation or liability on a Founder, Foundation or any other party in relation to the
Foundation or the property of the Foundation.
Subject to Article 14(3), a transfer of property to a Foundation shall not be void, voidable or liable
(2)
to be set aside by reason of a Founder or a Contributor's bankruptcy, the liquidation of a Founder
or Contributor, or any action or claims made against a Founder or Contributor by any creditor,
notwithstanding any foreign statute providing otherwise.

Notwithstanding Article 14(2), where the Court determines that:
(3)
at the time when the property was transferred to the Foundation, the Founder or
(a)

at the time such transfer took place the transfer of the property rendered the Founder or
(b)

then such transfer shall not be void or voidable or liable to be set aside and the Foundation shall be

Where a Foundation is liable to satisfy a creditor's claim in the manner provided for in Article 14(3),
(4)
that creditor's rights to recovery shall be limited to the property referred to in that Article, or to the
proceeds of that property, to the exclusion of any claim, right or action against the Foundation or
any other property of the Foundation.

affecting the ownership of immovable property in the DIFC and movable property including a

constituting an obligation or liability for any purpose.

FOUNDATIONS LAW
Assertion of jurisdiction over Foundation Officer
16A.
If any action is brought against a person in their capacity as a Foundation Officer in a foreign court
(1)
or tribunal having authority over the Foundation Officer, and such foreign court or tribunal fails to
dismiss such action and orders the Foundation Officer to take an action in regards to the
administration of such Foundation or in respect of such Foundation's property, the Foundation
Officer shall immediately upon the action of such court or tribunal and without the further order of
any court, cease in all respects to act in their capacity as a Foundation Officer or to have any
authority in respect of the Foundation.
Upon a Foundation Officer ceasing to hold office or have authority in relation to the Foundation in
(2)
accordance with Article 16A(1), such person's power or authority in regards to the Foundation shall
cease and such person, shall immediately, following its removal or loss of authority hereunder, give
notice in writing of such removal to the person or persons having the power to appoint a replacement
Foundation Officer, as applicable.

FOUNDATIONS LAW
PART 3: ESTABLISHMENT OF A DIFC FOUNDATION
Creation
17.
The Founder(s) may apply for the establishment of a Foundation by signing and filing with the
(1)
Registrar an application for its establishment in the manner prescribed by the Registrar.
The application filed with the Registrar under Article 17(1) shall be signed by each Founder and
(2)
shall include:
the name of the proposed Foundation;
(a)
the address of the proposed Foundation's registered office in the DIFC;
(b)
the full name, nationality and address of each Founder;
(c)
the full name, nationality and address of each of the proposed members of the Council of
(d)
the proposed Foundation;
if applicable, the name and business address of the proposed Registered Agent in the DIFC;
(e)
the Charter of the proposed Foundation;
(f)
unless a Registered Agent is proposed to be appointed, the By-laws of the proposed
(g)
Foundation; and
such other particulars as the Registrar may require.
(h)
A Foundation shall not use a name which does not comply with the requirements prescribed in the
(3)
Operating Law.
A person may not be named as a Registered Agent unless that person is a Qualified Person.
(4)
Upon lodgement of the application and payment of the prescribed fee, the Registrar shall either:
(5)
register the Foundation;
(a)
seek further information in respect of the proposed Foundation; or
(b)
refuse the application.
(c)
On the registration of a Foundation, the Registrar shall:
(6)
issue a certificate that the Foundation is established and such certificate shall expressly set
(a)
out its status as a Foundation:
assign to the Foundation a number, which shall be the Foundation's registered number; and
(b)
issue a Licence in its favour.
(c)
A Foundation shall hold a Licence pursuant to Article 9 of the Operating Law.
(7)
18.

its Charter, and
(a)
subject to Article 20(4), its By-laws.
(b)

FOUNDATIONS LAW
Charter
19.
A Foundation must have a Charter which shall be in the English language.
(1)
The Charter must contain the following:
(2)
its name;
(a)
the object(s);
(b)
a description of the initial capital;
(c)
the duration of the Foundation (if it is to subsist for a limited period only); and
(d)
a declaration by each Founder requesting the Council to comply with the terms of the
(e)
Charter.
The Charter may contain any matter:
(3)
that is otherwise required or permitted to be in the By-laws in accordance with Article 20;
(a)
or
that the Founder(s) think fit,
(b)
provided that there is no provision which is contrary to or inconsistent with this Law or the
(i)
Regulations.
The Charter may be amended only if:
(4)
the Charter so provides; or
(a)
the Court so orders under Article 41.
(b)
Where a Charter specifies an exclusively charitable object, it may not provide for the amendment of
(5)
the Charter so as to specify a non-charitable object in place of the charitable object.
Each Founder must subscribe to the Charter, if a Founder is:
(6)
an individual, by signing the Charter in his own name; or
(a)
a body corporate, by an authorised signatory signing the Charter.
(b)
The Board of Directors of the DIFCA may prescribe in the Regulations model provisions to be
(7)
known as the "Standard Charter", and a Foundation may, for its Charter, adopt the whole or any part
thereof as are applicable to that Foundation.
If a Foundation proposes to amend its Charter, it must after completing its internal processes
(8)
necessary to amend the Charter, lodge the amended Charter with the Registrar within a period of
thirty (30) days after such amendment. A Foundation which fails to comply with this requirement is
liable to a fine, as set out in Schedule 3.
The amended Charter shall come into force upon it being registered by the Registrar, and, if the
(9)
change involves a change of name, upon the issue of a certificate of name change by the Registrar.
The Charter may provide that if at any time the property of the Foundation includes any property
(10)
which by reason of the law of the UAE or any other specified jurisdiction may be held only by a

21.
Default recipient
The Charter or By-laws may identify a default recipient to whom all property of a Foundation for
(1)
which provision has not otherwise been made shall pass in the event of the termination thereof.

(2)

(b)

otherwise, become property of the DIFCA.
22.
Council
A Foundation must have a Council to administer its property and to carry out its objects.
(1)
The Council shall comprise at least two (2) members.
(2)
A Founder or a body corporate may be appointed as a member of the Council.
(3)
A member of the Council:
(4)
must be so named in the Register;
(a)
must ensure that the Council keeps accurate Accounting Records for the Foundation; and
(b)
must comply with the provisions of this Law, the Charter and By-laws.
(c)
A person must not be appointed as a member of a Council, or be so referred to in the Register, unless
(5)
he has consented in writing to being a member of the Council.
The appointment of a person as a member of a Council is void and will have no effect if that person
(6)
is also a Guardian of the Foundation.
The members of a Council must conduct the Foundation's affairs in accordance with its Charter and
(7)
By-laws, this Law and any other applicable law.

(b)
exercise of any power, where the person attempting to demand, request, approve, veto, compel the

FOUNDATIONS LAW
PART 4: PROPERTY OF A FOUNDATION
27.
Capital endowment
The initial capital of a Foundation is the capital endowed upon the Foundation in order that the
(1)
Foundation may be established.
The initial capital may comprise any property, and may be provided by way of gift or for valuable
(2)
consideration.
Following the endowment of the initial capital, further property may be endowed upon the
(3)
Foundation by any person if the Charter so permits.
A Founder does not have any interest in a Foundation by virtue only of endowing it with its initial
(4)
capital or further property or otherwise by virtue of being a Founder thereof.
No person has any interest in a Foundation, or is a Founder of a Foundation, by virtue only of
(5)
endowing it with further property in accordance with Article 27(3).
Financial Resources
28.
The property of a Foundation shall consist of:
the initial capital of the Foundation;
(a)
any further amount endowed upon the Foundation and accepted by its Council;
(b)
the proceeds of investment of the capital of the Foundation; and
(c)
any other property acquired by its Council in accordance with the Law and Regulations.
(d)

(2)
a person holding an entitlement specified in, or pursuant to, the By-laws to a fixed share of
(a)
the property and income of the Foundation when the Foundation distributes it;
a person holding a depository receipt;
(b)
(c)
a person who is a prospective recipient of a fixed, or discretionary, share of the property of
the Foundation upon the happening of a future event specified in the By-laws;
a person who is nominated pursuant to the By-laws to be a recipient of a fixed, or
(d)
discretionary, share of the property and income of the Foundation at a time following the
establishment of the Foundation;
a charity; and
(e)
a default recipient.
(f)
A Qualified Recipient has no right to or interest in the property of the Foundation other than a right
(3)
to payment of amounts which arises by virtue of the terms of the By-laws or pursuant to the By-
laws, or a contract with the Foundation, including a contract in relation to a depository receipt.

PART 5: ADMINISTRATION OF DIFC FOUNDATIONS
Conduct of business in the DIFC
32.
A Foundation shall, comply with the requirements of this Law and Part 3 of the Operating Law in respect to
the conduct of its business in the DIFC.
33.
Accounts and Accounting Records
Unless inconsistent with this Article 33, Articles 122 to 124 of the Companies Law shall apply to a
(1)
Foundation, as if it were a Company and any reference to "Director(s)" or "Officer(s)" in relevant
Articles in the Companies Law shall be interpreted to refer to member(s) of the Council. A
Foundation which fails to keep and maintain accounts or make them available as required under this
Law is liable to a fine, as set out in Schedule 3.
A Foundation's accounts shall be approved by the Council and signed on their behalf by at least two
(2)
members of the Council. A Foundation which fails to comply with this requirement is liable to a
fine, as set out in Schedule 3.
A Foundation shall within thirty (30) days after the accounts have been approved by the Council:
(3)
if it has not appointed a Registered Agent, file a copy of its annual accounts with the
(a)
Registrar; or
if it has appointed a Registered Agent, provide a copy of its annual accounts to its
(b)
Registered Agent.
A Foundation which fails to comply with the requirements in Article 33(3) is liable to a fine, as set
(4)
out in Schedule 3.
A person who makes a statement that is false, misleading or deceptive in a material way to the
(5)
Registrar or the Registered Agent in respect of accounts, is liable to a fine as set out in Schedule 3.

(1)
upon him by:
Articles 6 and 7 of the Operating Law; and
(a)
Articles 22 to 33 of the Operating Law,
(b)
including the power of delegation.

any document, form or fee required by this Law or the Relevant Laws to be lodged with or

may be lodged with or paid to the Registrar by the Registered Agent; and
the Registered Agent is required to perform some or all assessments, checks and
(b)

whether an applicant or Foundation:

(i)
of the Relevant Laws; and
maintains Accounting Records in accordance with Article 33,
(ii)
and to provide a certification of such matters to the Registrar in an agreed format.

(3)
without further enquiry, upon the certification of a Registered Agent given under Article 34(2),
provided that a Registered Agent shall not be liable to the Registrar for doing so, unless it has acted
in breach of its agreed obligations to the Registrar, in which case (without limiting the generality of
its application) Article 34(4) shall apply.
The Registrar may:
(4)
revoke any arrangement entered into with a Registered Agent under Article 34(2) if the
(a)

to the Registrar;
inform the DFSA of any facts or circumstances that may amount to a breach of a Registered
(b)
Agent's regulatory duties under applicable law; and
inform law enforcement agencies of any alleged or suspected criminal offences on the part
(c)
of a Registered Agent or its clients.
Where an applicant is represented by a Registered Agent in an application to establish or continue a
(5)
Foundation, the Registrar shall be permitted to communicate directly with such Registered Agent
with no need to directly communicate with the applicant pursuant to this Law.
35.

The Registrar must keep a Register for the purposes of this Law.

details of any changes filed with the Registrar (other than the nationality and address of any natural

PART 7: JUDICIAL AND NON JUDICIAL PROCEEDINGS
39.
Role of the Court in administration of Foundations
The Court may intervene in the administration of a Foundation to the extent its jurisdiction is
(1)
invoked by a Person with Sufficient Interest or as provided by Law.
A Foundation is not subject to continuing judicial supervision unless so ordered by the Court.
(2)
Unless specifically stated otherwise, any Person with Sufficient Interest in respect of a Foundation
(3)
may apply to the Court to make any order or take any action specified in this Part.
If the Court has to determine whether a person is a Person with Sufficient Interest in respect of a
(4)
Foundation, the reference in Article 39(1) to a Person with Sufficient Interest, for the purpose of
determining that question, shall include such a person.
Power of the Court to order compliance
40.
If the Court is satisfied that a person has failed to comply with:
(1)
a requirement of this Law, the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation; or
(a)
an obligation imposed on the person by this Law, the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation,
(b)
the Court may order the person to comply with the requirement or obligation.
(d)
If the Court is satisfied that a Foundation, acting through its Council, has failed to carry out its
(2)
objects or any of them, the Court may order the Foundation to do so.
An order under this Article may specify the action the person or Foundation is required to take.
(3)
41.
Power of the Court to order amendment of Charter or By-laws
The Court may on an application made by or on behalf of a Founder, the Foundation or a Guardian
(1)
order the amendment of the Charter or the By-laws of a Foundation, if the Court is satisfied:
that the change will assist the Foundation to administer its property or to attain its objects;
(a)
or
that those objects are no longer attainable and that the change will assist the Foundation to
(b)
attain objects as near as reasonably possible to those objects.
If the order is to amend a Foundation's Charter, the thirty (30) day period referred to Article 19(8)
(2)
to file an amended Charter with the Registrar shall be calculated from the date of the Court order.
Without limiting Article 41(1), the Court may make an order under Article 41(1) in the event that a
(3)
Foundation does not have By-laws, or that the relevant provisions of the By-laws cannot be readily
ascertained or are otherwise incomplete.
The Court may, on an application made by or on behalf of a Founder, in its absolute discretion and
(4)
on such terms and conditions as it thinks fit, by order authorise the rectification of any error, defect
or omission in the Charter or the By-laws of a Foundation.
42.
Power of the Court to give directions
This Article applies if the Court is satisfied:
(1)
that if it gives a direction it will assist a Foundation to administer its property or to carry
(a)
out its objects; or
22

The Court may, in addition to giving a direction under Article 42(2), make such an order as it thinks

43.
Power of the Court to protect interests under a Foundation
The Court may in any proceeding under this Part by order appoint a person to protect the interests
(1)
of a person who the Court is satisfied is a Qualified Recipient under a Foundation, where the Court
is satisfied that the person is unable to act on his, her or its own behalf and may by order determine
the terms of such appointment, remove such person and give directions as to service of such person.
A person so appointed may represent the person whose interests he has been appointed to protect in
(2)
any dealing with the Foundation or in any proceeding under this Part.
Where a representative has been appointed under Article 43(1), no settlement affecting a person
(3)
to whom Article 43(1) applies shall be valid without the approval of the Court.
Power of the Court to dismiss or appoint a Registered Agent
44.
If the Court is satisfied that it is in the interest of a Foundation to do so, it may order:
(1)
the removal of its Registered Agent; and/or
(a)
the appointment of a Qualified Person to be its Registered Agent.
(b)
The Foundation must give notice of the change of Registered Agent to the Registrar within thirty
(2)
(30) days of the order of the Court under Article 44(1). A Foundation which fails to comply with
this requirement is liable to a fine, as set out in Schedule 3.
Power of the Court to take action on behalf of others
45.
This Article applies where the Court is satisfied that a person has failed to comply with:
(1)
a requirement of this Law, the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation; or
(a)
an obligation imposed on the person by this Law, the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation.
(b)
The Court may, by order, require the Registrar comply with the requirement or obligation on behalf
(2)
of the person who has failed to do so.

In this Article, 'person exercising a power' means a person who exercises a power to transfer or
(1)
make other disposition of property to a Foundation on behalf of a Founder and who owes a fiduciary
duty to such Founder in relation to the exercise of his or her power.

(3)

failed to take into account any relevant considerations or took into account irrelevant

would not have exercised the power, or would not have exercised the power in the way it
(b)

taking into account of irrelevant considerations.
This Article applies whether or not the circumstances set out in Article 48(3) occurred as a result of
(4)
any lack of care or other fault on the part of the person exercising a power, or on the part of any
person giving advice in relation to the exercise of the power.

FOUNDATIONS LAW
Provisions for facilitating Foundation division or amalgamation
50.
This Article applies where the members of the Council of one or more Foundations unanimously
(1)
resolve that the property of a Foundation should be divided amongst two (2) or more Foundations,
or that two (2) or more Foundations should be combined into a single Foundation.
An application may made to the Court under Article 50(1) for the division of the property of the
(2)
Foundation, or the amalgamation of the Foundations, on notice to all Persons with Sufficient Interest
in any Foundation affected by the making of such an order.
On any application under Article 50(1), the Court may make such orders as it considers appropriate
(3)
to facilitate the division or amalgamation, including:
establishing one or more new Foundations;
(a)
appointing the members of the Council for each of such Foundations;
(b)
determining that the existing members of the Council shall continue to act as members of
(c)
the Council or Councils for one or more of such Foundations; or
determining that the existing Foundation will continue in accordance with its existing
(d)
Charter and By-laws and the other Foundation or Foundations will be established with
similar or different Charter(s) or By-laws approved by the Court.
The Court may take into consideration the difference in tax attributes and other pertinent factors in
(4)
administering the property of any separate Foundation, in making applicable tax elections, and in
making applications or distributions.
A separate Foundation created pursuant to this Article 50 must be treated as a separate Foundation
(5)
for all purposes from the date on which the order of the Court is made, including, but not limited to
any and all issues related to the liability of the Foundation or the property of the Foundation pursuant
to a contract, in tort or otherwise.
In dividing the property of a Foundation between two (2) or more separate Foundations, the Court
(6)
shall accomplish the division by dividing the property on a fractional basis and transferring any
property not retained by the existing Foundation to the other Foundations either:
with a pro rata portion of each asset forming part of the property of the existing Foundation;
(a)
or
on a non-pro rata basis based on either the fair market value of the assets on the date of the
(b)
order or in a manner that fairly reflects the net appreciation or depreciation in the value of
the assets measured from the valuation date to the date of funding.
Each of the Foundations affected by any order of the Court and any Foundation established by order
(7)
of the Court shall give details of the change of status or establishment of the Foundations to the
Registrar in such manner as he may require.
The Registrar shall make such entries in the Register as are required to give effect to the order of the
(8)
Court and ensure any new or amalgamated Foundation is correctly registered.
51.
Arbitration of Foundation disputes
Where the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation provide that any dispute or Administration Question
(1)
arising between any of the parties in relation to the Foundation shall be submitted to arbitration, that
provision shall, for all purposes under the Arbitration Law have effect as between those parties as if
it were an arbitration agreement and as if those parties were parties to that agreement.
Where the Charter or By-laws of a Foundation do not provide that any dispute or Administration
(2)
Question arising in relation to the Foundation shall be submitted to arbitration but the parties to that

(2)

61.
Discontinuance and effect
Upon receipt of a notice satisfactory to him that a Foundation that has made an application under
(1)
Article 59 has been continued as an entity under the laws of another jurisdiction, the Registrar must
file the notice and issue a certificate of discontinuance in accordance with this Law.
After a certificate of discontinuance is issued under Article 61(1) in respect of a Foundation that is
(2)
continued as an entity under the laws of another jurisdiction, the Foundation's name shall be
removed from the Register, but such removal does not affect the application of any Foreign Law
referred to in Article 60.
62.
Recognised Foreign Foundations
A Foreign Foundation which wishes to conduct operations within the DIFC may apply for a Licence
(1)
to be a Recognised Foreign Foundation in the DIFC for the purposes of this Law, in accordance with
Article 9 of the Operating Law.
An application made under Article 62(1) shall be in such form as may be prescribed by the Registrar
(2)
and accompanied by the prescribed fee.
A Recognised Foreign Foundation must appoint a Registered Agent, have a registered office in the
(3)
DIFC and comply with the requirements of this Law and where applicable, Part 3 of the Operating
Law, in respect to the conduct of its business in the DIFC.
A Recognised Foreign Foundation may not carry out any commercial activities, except those
(4)
necessary for, and ancillary or incidental to, its objects.
Article 24 and Part 5 of this Law and Articles 133 to 139 of the Companies Law shall apply with all
(5)
necessary modifications to a Recognised Foreign Foundation, including that it shall not be necessary
for a Recognised Foreign Foundation to appoint an Approved Person.
63.
Continuation of Company as a DIFC Foundation
A Company incorporated under the Companies Law may, if authorized by a unanimous resolution
(1)
of all of its shareholders, apply to the Registrar for a certificate of continuance under this Law.
An application made under Article 63(1) shall be in such form as may be prescribed by the Registrar
(2)
and be accompanied by a Charter of Continuance, and, if the application does not propose the
appointment of a Registered Agent, the By-laws (if any) that will apply to the Company upon its
continuance as a Foundation under this Law.
A Charter of Continuance and proposed By-laws (if any) shall effect any amendment to the
(3)
memorandum and Articles of Association of the Company that applies for continuance under this
Article necessary to make the Charter and By-laws conform to this Law and any other relevant law
applicable in the DIFC, and may make any other amendments which are permitted by this Law.

65.
Certificate of continuance of a Company
Upon receipt of the Charter of Continuance and, if applicable, the proposed By-laws, the Registrar
(1)
shall if he is satisfied that Articles 63 and 64 have been complied with, issue a certificate of
continuance in respect of the Company which shall also serve as the certificate of establishment of
the Company as a Foundation in the DIFC.
On the date shown in the certificate of continuance:
(2)
the Company shall become a Foundation in the DIFC to which this Law shall apply as if it
(a)
had been established under this Law;
the Charter of Continuance shall become the Charter thereof in the place of its
(b)
memorandum and Articles of Association; and
the Company shall cease to be registered as a Company in the DIFC.
(c)
66.
Preservation of a Company as a Foundation
When a Company is continued as a Foundation in the DIFC under this Law:
the property of the Company becomes the property of the Foundation;
(a)
the Foundation continues to be liable for the obligations of the Company;
(b)
an existing cause of action, claim or liability to prosecution is unaffected;
(c)
a civil, criminal or administrative action or proceeding pending by or against the Company may be
(d)
continued by or against the Foundation; and
(e)
a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment against or in favour of, the Company may be
enforced against the Foundation.
66A.
Continuation of DIFC Foundation as a Company
A DIFC Foundation registered pursuant to this Law may, if it is:
(1)
permitted under its Charter and By-laws to do so; and
(a)
authorised by a unanimous resolution of its Council Members,
(b)
apply to the Registrar for a certificate of continuance under the Companies Law.
An application made under Article 66A(1) shall be in such form as may be prescribed by the
(2)
Registrar and be accompanied by Articles of Continuation that comply with Articles 11(1) and 11(2)
of the Companies Law, the written agreement of the proposed shareholders of the Company to hold
the shares referred to in Article 66B(2)(c) and any other Document prescribed by the Registrar.
The Articles of Continuation shall make any amendments to the Charter and By-laws of the DIFC
(3)
Foundation necessary to make the Articles of Continuation conform to the Companies Law and any
other relevant law applicable in the DIFC, and may make any other amendments which are permitted
by the Companies Law.
32

FOUNDATIONS LAW
PART 9: DISSOLUTION OF FOUNDATIONS
Dissolution
67.
A Foundation shall be dissolved where:
(1)
the Foundation is established for a definite period and that period expires;
(a)
the object(s) of the Foundation are fulfilled or become incapable of fulfillment, and the
(b)
members of the Council, by unanimous decision, so resolve;
any provision of the Charter or By-laws of the Foundation so requires;
(c)
the Court orders that the Foundation be dissolved under Article 68; or
(d)
the Registrar strikes the Foundation off the Register in accordance with Article 71.
(e)
Where a Foundation is dissolved pursuant to the provisions of Article 67(1), the members of the
(2)
Council, or such other person as may be authorized by the Charter or By-laws of the Foundation to
supervise the dissolution of the Foundation, shall do all things that are necessary for orderly
supervision of dissolution of the Foundation, and shall collect the property of the Foundation, and
after discharging or making adequate provision for the discharge of the obligations of the Foundation
shall distribute the remaining property in the manner provided in Article 69.
68.
Court dissolution
A Foundation may, upon application, by an order of the Court be dissolved if the Court is of the
(1)
opinion that:
the Foundation is insolvent;
(a)
it is just and equitable that the Foundation be dissolved; or
(b)
it is prejudicial to the interest of the DIFC for a Foundation to remain on the Register.
(c)
An application for the dissolution of a Foundation under Article 68(1)(a) or (b) may be made by the
(2)
Foundation, a member of the Council of the Foundation, a Guardian of the Foundation or by a
creditor of the Foundation.
An application for the dissolution of a Foundation under Article 68(1)(c) may be made by the
(3)
DIFCA.
Where the Court orders that a Foundation be dissolved under this Article, the Court may appoint a
(4)
person to supervise the dissolution of the Foundation and may, from time to time, direct the manner
in which the dissolution is to be conducted.
69.
Distribution of property
Subject to Article 69(2), where a Foundation is dissolved and there remains some property after its
(1)
dissolution such property shall be the property of the person who, according to the Charter or By-
laws, is entitled to receive any property remaining after the dissolution of the Foundation.
In the event that:
(2)
there is no person entitled to receive the remaining property of the Foundation as provided
(a)
in Article 69(1); or
the person entitled to receive the remaining property refuses to accept the transfer of the
(b)
property,
34

FOUNDATIONS LAW
and there is no relevant provision in the Charter or By-laws of the Foundation, the remaining
property shall vest in the DIFCA.
70.
Application of insolvency rules in winding up
Unless the Regulations provide otherwise, in the winding up of an insolvent Foundation the same
(1)
rules concerning rights of creditors, proof of debts, valuation of liabilities and preferential payments
as are in operation for the time being under the Insolvency Law with respect to the estates of
insolvent companies apply and must be observed.
The Regulations may provide for the manner in which the Insolvency Law will apply to the winding
(2)
up of insolvent Foundations.
71.
Dissolution by the Registrar
If the Registrar has reason to believe that:
(1)
a Foundation is acting in contravention of this Law and the Regulations; or
(a)
it is prejudicial to the interest of the DIFC for a Foundation to remain on the Register,
(b)
he may give notice to the Foundation that at the conclusion of three (3) months from the date of the
notice, the Foundation shall be struck off the Register unless reason is shown to the contrary.
If by the end of the three (3) month period, the Registrar has not received from the Foundation an
(2)
objection to the striking off of the Foundation from the Register or from any other party sufficient
reason as to why the Foundation should not be struck off the Register, the Registrar may strike the
name of the Foundation off the Register and the Foundation shall be dissolved.
When a Foundation has been struck off the Register under Article 71(2), the Foundation or its
(3)
liquidator may apply to have the name of the Foundation reinstated on the Register, provided such
application is made prior to the sixth anniversary of its removal from the Register.
Where an application is made under Article 71(3) and upon payment of all outstanding fees and
(4)
penalties to the Registrar, the Registrar may reinstate the Foundation on the Register.
Where the Foundation is reinstated on the Register under Article 71(4), the Foundation is deemed
(5)
to have continued in existence as if it had not been dissolved or struck off the Register.
Upon dissolution of a Foundation under this Article, its outstanding property shall become the
(6)
property of the DIFCA, provided that if it is reinstated to the Register such property shall once again
become the property of the Foundation.
72.
Publication of dissolution
The Registrar shall publish the dissolution of a Foundation on the website of the DIFC.
35

(d)

(h)
year" shall mean a year of the Gregorian calendar;
a reference to the masculine gender includes the feminine;
(i)
the singular shall include the plural and vice versa; and
()
"dollar" or "$" is a reference to United States Dollars unless the contrary intention appears.
(k)
The headings in the Law shall not affect its interpretation.

A reference in this Law to a Part, Chapter, Article or Schedule by number only, and without further
(3)

FOUNDATIONS LAW
SCHEDULE 2
APPLICATION OF THE ARBITRATION LAW
1.
The Arbitration Law shall apply and be construed with respect to a Foundation arbitration, as stated
hereunder.
2.
In the Arbitration Law, "dispute" includes an Administration Question.
3.
Article 12(1) of the Arbitration Law shall apply as if it read:
"A Founder or Contributor of a Foundation shall be free to determine (by provision in the Charter or By-
laws of the Foundation) how, in relation to a Foundation, disputes are resolved, subject only to such
safeguards as are necessary in the public interest;"
Where in the Arbitration Law reference is made to a matter agreed between the parties to an arbitration
4.
agreement (including a matter which may be authorised, chosen, conferred, designated, nominated or vested
by the parties) that matter shall (except where no effective provision is made) be determined as provided in
the Foundation's Charter or By-laws.
5.
Neither Article 12 of the Arbitration Law nor any rule of law or construction treating an arbitration agreement
separate to any agreement of which it is a part shall apply in relation to a Foundation arbitration.
6.
The term "action" in Article 13 of the Arbitration Law includes an application or other reference to the
Court concerning an Administration Question which the Charter or By-laws of the Foundation requires to
be submitted to arbitration and a stay of that application or other reference may be sought by any of the
parties in relation to the Foundation, whether or not a party to that application or other reference.
7.
In any application or other reference to the Court referred to in paragraph 6, the Court may stay the
proceedings on its own volition unless all parties in relation to the Foundation affected by the application
are before it or are represented by persons before it.
8.
For the objects of enforcing an arbitral award underArticle 41(2)(a)(iii) of the Arbitration Law, the term
"dispute" includes an Administration Question.
42

| 10. | Nature of a Foundation |
| --- | --- |
| (1) | A Foundation is a body corporate with a legal personality separate from that of its Founder(s) and |
| (2) | A Foundation has the capacity, rights and privileges of a natural person. The validity of an act done |
| (3) | The property of a Foundation is not held by it upon trust for any other person. |
| (4) | A Founder has such rights (if any) in respect of a Foundation as provided for in its By-laws. |
| (5) | A person specified in the By-laws (other than a Founder, a member of the Council, the Registered |
| (6) | Any rights a person may have in respect of a Foundation may be assigned to some other person, if |
| (7) | If rights are assigned under Article 10(6), the person assigning the rights must within a period of |
| 11. | Duration of a Foundation |
| (1) | A Foundation may, but need not, be established for a fixed period or for a specified limited period, |
| (2) | If a Foundation is to be wound up and dissolved upon: |
| (a) | the happening of some event; or |
| (b) | the expiration of a fixed period of time, |
| (c) | the details of the event or period must be specified in its By-laws. |
| (3) | If a person is to have the right to wind up and dissolve a Foundation, the details of such right must |
| 12. | Objects and categories of Foundations |
| (1) | A Foundation's objects: |
| (a) | must be certain, reasonable and possible; and |
| (b) | must not be unlawful or contrary to public policy in the DIFC. |
| (2) | A Foundation may be established for: |
| (a) | objects which are exclusively charitable; or |
| (b) | one or more of the following: |
| (i) | objects which are not exclusively charitable; or |
| (ii) | objects to benefit persons by name, category or class. |

- p10: FOUNDATIONS LAW. | (1) | The Founder(s) may apply for the establishment of a Foundation by signing and filing with the |
| (2) | The application filed with the Registrar under Article 17(1) shall be signed by each Founder and |
| (a) | the name of the proposed Foundation; |
| (b) | the address of the proposed Foundation's registered office in the DIFC; |
| (c) | the full name, nationality and address of each Founder; |

| (h) | such other particulars as the Registrar may require. |
| (3) | A Foundation shall not use a name which does not comply with the requirements prescribed in the |
| (4) | A person may not be named as a Registered Agent unless that person is a Qualified Person. |
| (5) | Upon lodgement of the application and payment of the prescribed fee, the Registrar shall either: |
| (a) | register the Foundation; |
| (b) | seek further information in respect of the proposed Foundation; or |
| (c) | refuse the application. |
| (6) | On the registration of a Foundation, the Registrar shall: |
| (a) | issue a certificate that the Foundation is established and such certificate shall expressly set |
| (b) | assign to the Foundation a number, which shall be the Foundation's registered number; and |
| (c) | issue a Licence in its favour. |
| (7) | A Foundation shall hold a Licence pursuant to Article 9 of the Operating Law. |

| (2) | The Charter must contain the following: |

| (b) | the object(s); |

| (a) | the Charter so provides; or |
| (b) | the Court so orders under Article 41. |
| (5) | Where a Charter specifies an exclusively charitable object, it may not provide for the amendment of |
| (6) | Each Founder must subscribe to the Charter, if a Founder is: |

| (b) | a body corporate, by an authorised signatory signing the Charter. |
| (7) | The Board of Directors of the DIFCA may prescribe in the Regulations model provisions to be |

| 22. | Council |
| (1) | A Foundation must have a Council to administer its property and to carry out its objects. |
| (2) | The Council shall comprise at least two (2) members. |
| (3) | A Founder or a body corporate may be appointed as a member of the Council. |
| (4) | A member of the Council: |
| (a) | must be so named in the Register; |
| (b) | must ensure that the Council keeps accurate Accounting Records for the Foundation; and |
| (c) | must comply with the provisions of this Law, the Charter and By-laws. |
| (5) | A person must not be appointed as a member of a Council, or be so referred to in the Register, unless |

| 36. | Keeping of records by Registrar |
| (1) | A record delivered to the Registrar under this Law may be kept by the Registrar in any form: |
| (a) | that is approved by the Board of Directors of the DIFCA; and |
| (b) | that is capable of being reproduced in a legible form. |
| (2) | The Registrar is to be taken to have complied with an obligation to keep a record if he has complied |
| (3) | The Registrar may destroy a record kept by the Registrar if: |
| (a) | it is an original record and it has been recorded and kept in accordance with Article 36(1); |
| (b) | it relates solely to a Foundation that has been dissolved more than six (6) years previously. |
| 37. | Registrar may change registration number of Foundation |
| 38. | Power of the Registrar to refuse Documents |
| (1) | The Registrar may refuse to accept any Document delivered under this Law if: |
| (a) | it does not comply with this Law or the Regulations; |
| (b) | it has not been duly completed; |
| (c) | it contains any material error; |
| (d) | it is not legible; or |
| (e) | is not accompanied by the prescribed fee. |

| 53A. | Limitation of actions |
| (1) | No action or proceeding whether pursuant to this Law or at common law or in equity: |
| (a) | to set aside the establishment of a Foundation; |
| (b) | to set aside any disposition to any Foundation; or |
| (c) | for any order under this Part, |
| (i) | the date of the establishment of the Foundation that is sought to be set aside; |
| (ii) | the disposition to the Foundation that is sought to be set aside; or |
| (iii) | the right to commence the action or proceeding arising as the case may be. |
| (2) | No action or proceeding whether pursuant to this Law or at common law or in equity shall be |
| (a) | claiming to have had an interest in property before that property was settled upon or |
| (b) | seeking to derive a legal or equitable interest in that property, |

| 54. | Continuation of a Foreign Foundation in the DIFC |

| (1) | A Foreign Foundation may, if not prohibited in terms of its founding Documents or under the laws |

| 55. | Charter of Continuance for Foreign Foundations |
| (1) | The Charter of Continuance of a Foreign Foundation shall be written in the English language. |
| (2) | The Charter of Continuance of a Foreign Foundation shall: |
| (a) | be signed by all of the members of the Council or substantially equivalent officers of the |
| (b) | state the name of the Foreign Foundation and the name under which it will be continued in |

| (d) | state the date on which it was established; and |
| (e) | comply with such other requirements under this Law as apply to the Charter. |
| 56. | Certificate of continuance for Foreign Foundations |
| (1) | Upon receipt of the Charter of Continuance and, if applicable, the proposed By-laws, the Registrar |

| (b) | the Charter of Continuance shall become the Charter thereof. |
